# Tries ledger: every change tried for an entry, per test board

Started 2026-11-03 by the AI assistant under the captain's delegation of 2026-11-02, after the captain asked for
continuous iteration with no fixed timeline and a side agent warned that endless tweaking against the same few
backtests lets lucky fits through even when the adoption rule passes. The rule's 10 seeds rule out draw noise, not a
change that only suits the particular released stages a backtest uses.

From 2026-11-04 the ledger also carries the captain's guiding principle (quoted in `AGENTS.md`): rules 5-8 below and
the headroom tables of where the entries stand against the published baselines, written by the AI assistant under the
same delegation. Rows written before that date stay as they were.

## Rules

1. Every change tried for an entry gets a row here **before its run**: the biological or physical reason, the metrics
   where its gain should show, and what would make a gain a fit to one backtest rather than a real effect. The row is
   committed before the run starts; the row names that commit.
2. A change is kept only if all three hold:
   - the adoption rule passes (`vec.adoption`, `docs/PLAN.md` section 3, item 8);
   - the gain sits on the metrics its reason predicts;
   - no known feature of one backtest explains it. Example: the E7.25 embryo target sits much closer to E6.75 than
     its day says (2026-11-03 diagnostic below), so a change that moves the mixture toward the earlier bracket gains
     there and says nothing about E7.75, where the bracket fraction is 1/2. A second (added 2026-11-04 from the
     rebuild design, wf_237f8d8f-e7c): by mmd_u both embryo backtests prefer equal mixing to their nominal
     fraction (0.0152 against 0.0264 on E7.25, 0.0380 against 0.0433 on E7.5; the 2026-11-03 table below), so a
     change that pulls either backtest's mixture toward 1/2 gains on both and says nothing at test, where the
     nominal fraction is already 1/2.
3. Changes without a reason that holds at test are pruned before they run, with the reason written down.
4. Every try counts, diagnostics included; each value of a swept parameter is one try. The counts go into every
   adoption report.
5. **The baseline and the question.** Every row, written before its run, names its board's published baseline
   (copy_last on T1, the heart and the embryo; wt_identity on T3) and the entry or candidate it changes. It answers the
   captain's question: what meaningful change beyond the baseline does it predict, and what held-out evidence would
   show it? The change names its facet: which states emerge, their proportions, their expression or, on T2, their
   spatial organisation. The evidence is named in advance: the backtests or folds that count for it under the
   adoption rule, none it was tuned or selected on (on T3 the cross-knockout folds; the Gata4 replicate fold never
   counts). The row also states the gain it expects there; a change expected to gain less than the adoption rule's
   0.3 points on its counting backtests is pruned before it runs (rule 3), since it could pass only by luck. Its
   outcome reports the result against the floor as well as against the entry. No test-board score is evidence.
6. **Weakest open group first.** Every row names the scored group it targets by its `vec.boards.GROUPS` id, its weight
   and its line in the headroom tables ("Where the entries stand against the published baselines"). A board's next
   try targets its weakest group (the most points left on the counting backtests; `docs/PLAN.md` section 3, item 9)
   whose lever is open; a try on another group says why the weaker ones are closed or have no reason that holds at
   test.
   - **Ties.** When a board's counting backtests name different weakest groups, those groups count as tied; across
     boards, gaps within the spread of a board's stand-ins count as tied. The row says which it targets first and why.
   - **Closed levers.** A lever is closed by the post-exposure block (`docs/DATA_POLICY.md`, 2026-11-04, (b) to (d):
     cell numbers, sizes and scope on every board; T3's composition; T1's composition except by the legal route of
     (d)), by a ruling of the captain (the heart's composition trend, dropped on 2026-11-01, `docs/PLAN.md`
     4.3, pipeline step 2), by a standing working answer (no new cross-modal input enters an upload until the
     organisers answer, A15) or by this ledger after its tries (the depth, blend, herding and coupling lines; the
     captain can reopen one). A lever fixed by a frozen or pre-registered rule moves only as that rule allows or by the
     captain's own ruling: on T3 a factor change that passes the adoption rule on the cross-knockout folds goes to him
     (`docs/PLAN.md` decision log 2026-11-01 (d)), and `experiments/t3_decomp/rules.yaml` is not edited. A closed
     lever stays closed whatever its headroom.
7. **Developmental reasons.** A developmental reason makes a change testable: it names the metrics the change should
   move and on which backtests. It never keeps a step the adoption rule rejects, and a gain off the predicted metrics
   is not kept (rule 2). An extra part of a change (a parameter, a step, a data source) needs its own held-out gain,
   measured with and without it, each run counted (rule 4); without that gain it is dropped.
8. **The tables follow the entries.** After every kept change or new entry, the script is rerun from committed
   results only (`python3 -I experiments/harness/headroom.py`), its output in `results/headroom/` is committed, and
   the headroom tables are copied from `headroom.txt`, naming the commit they were built at. An entry the script does
   not cover yet is added to its board list first. No official test score enters the tables.

## Where the entries stand against the published baselines

Built on 2026-11-04 by `experiments/harness/headroom.py` from the committed results at fcd8964, and rerun on
2026-11-13 at c4501f5 after heart row H11 was kept (the heart's entry is now `b0_trend`; the other boards' lines are
unchanged) (stdlib only:
`python3 -I experiments/harness/headroom.py`; output in `results/headroom/headroom.txt` and `headroom.json`, with the
sha256 of its 19 inputs; the script and its output are added by the commit that adds this section). It reads
committed practice-task results only, never a target, a test-board file or a cell count, and it runs no candidate, so
it is not a try. Its self-checks: every stored board score recomputed from its skills to 1.4e-14 points, and every
mean used within 0.0005 of `results/registry.csv`.

Each cell gives a scored group's points against the published baseline, 100 x weight x (skill - 0.5), seed-paired,
then its points left to the split-half ceiling, 100 x weight x (1 - skill), on one backtest the adoption rule counts
for the entry (10 seeds). The baseline scores 50 on every seed of every backtest. * marks a stand-in, used where no
backtest can run the entry. "copy" is a fresh draw of the reference stage's real cells at the count rule, the
baseline as we would submit it: it is not the scorer's own working copy, so it can score below 50. The columns are
the `vec.boards.GROUPS` ids, which `headroom.txt` prints: T1 de_recovery, direction, distribution, covariance; T2
expression_change, cell_state, shape_growth, local_organisation (neighbourhoods); T3 response_identity,
response_direction, response_magnitude, mutant_state.

are a 0.30-weight metric at the floor. Points left in all ("Points left in Overall units per board"): T1 49.03, T3
39.23 (26.67 with stand-in A), heart 24.53, embryo 20.02.

Read with care:
- Points left are measured to the split-half ceiling, which no prediction reaches, and the skill map is 1/(1 + x), so
  equal points left are not equal reach. Near the floor (the M1 and H1 stand-ins, T3's s2) the ranking mostly
  restates the weights.
- These are backtests, not the test: T1 on E9.5 -> E10.5 (test E10.5 -> E12.5), whose stand-in draws E9.5's chamber
  mix against E10.5's chamber-cut truth; the heart 1 and 1.75 days ahead (test 2); the embryo at t = 1/3 and 2/3
  (test 1/2). Whether a backtest's weakest group is the test's is assumption A22.
- Earlier tries already aimed at weak groups (a check, not a change to their rows): T5 at B0's DE recovery on T1
  (de_score skill 0.450 on the chamber cut), H3 at the heart's cell state (-0.32 on the 1-day backtest), E7 and E8 at
  fgw's cell state (weak on E7.25 only), E11 at fgw's shape on E7.5 (its one group below the baseline there).

### What it means for the order of the next tries

1. **T1 composition by the legal route.** Births by the legal route were designed and pruned on 2026-11-04
   (wf_77e506c8-014; "Pruned before running"). `docs/DATA_POLICY.md` 2026-11-04 (d) then reopened T1's shares within the
   chamber scope by the legal route, and (f) says how a share rule is built (quotas at the frozen count, never
   importance weights; T1 depth closed). The rebuild design of 2026-11-04 (wf_237f8d8f-e7c) registered no composition
   try: no legal share rule has a counted check, since both counted T1 tasks draw E9.5's chamber mix (cardiomyocytes
   about 64% of cells, against about 19% in the chamber-cut truth: the pruned exact top-up below), and the committed
   oracle-base grid (`experiments/s2_hedge/`) puts the current s1/s2 pair within about 1.5 points of the best pair under
   every prior it states. A share change can enter only by the captain's ruling after he writes a prior (item 6). What
   remained on T1 was R1, a ruling request: cells without a Feng group keep their E10.5 expression (row below), kept by
   the captain's ruling of 2026-11-05. A later composition row still follows rules 5-8 and names that confound in
   advance.
2. **T3 response identity.** The largest gap in Overall units on the cross-knockout stand-in (15.18); on stand-in A,
   s1's own factor, it is 11.89, behind T1 distribution (15.08) and T1 DE recovery (13.00). So it ties with T1
   distribution (rule 6), and T1 went first; the rebuild design found no T1 try (item 1) and no T3 candidate with a
   reason that holds at test ("Pruned before running"). A factor change must pass the adoption rule on the two
   cross-knockout folds and then goes to the captain; the composition is closed. The one reading the principle asks
   for, s1's own lead component (the Gata4 factor, without composition) on the Gata4 -> Mab21l2 fold, the first
   reading of it on a knockout it was not fitted on (A10), is registered as K2a with a permuted-factor control K2b
   (rows below; decided by the AI assistant under the delegation of 2026-11-02, the captain can overrule). It changes
   no upload: T3's s1 changes only by the captain's ruling, whatever the outcome.
3. **Embryo: stop polishing.** It has the smallest gap (20.02 Overall points left) and the most tries (9), no group
   has an open lever with a reason that holds at test, and its one measured test risk, the flattened s1 geometry, is
   hedged by s2.
4. **Heart: no try.** Its entry is copy_last at the count rule plus a small count-space momentum, within 1.3 points of
   the baseline on every metric, and the pre-registered size, which no backtest can score. No lever on it has a
   reason that holds at test (above).
5. **T1 direction: no other try.** Feng's E9.5 lies inside every T1 window, and both T1 backtests that count for a
   same-scope change (the whole and the chamber-cut E10.5 truths) carry E9.5's chamber mix, so no check exists for a
   change to Feng's direction. (2026-11-17: superseded for labelled reads, as above; tries T44 to T47 and the rows
   after them.)
6. **After the first wave.** No further try is registered without a new reason that holds at test, written in its row
   before the run. The rebuild design's second-wave items run only at the captain's request: H5, a placebo-controlled
   reading of B0's momentum on both heart backtests (information only); D3, pricing T1's cardiomyocyte tail, which
   changes a frozen s2 rule and runs only after the captain writes a prior (under the stated priors it loses, 68.28
   against 67.89 with a uniform prior; break-even needs about 25% of the prior on a cardiomyocyte share of at least 0.8;
   not run: no prior was written by 2026-11-05, and it lowers the chance of a high total,
   `experiments/gate/options_tail.py`); T3's s2 moved to the barred branch's s1 (a frozen rule; it would override D11b's
   failed bar after the fact; not recommended; re-examined on 2026-11-05 and rejected after an adversarial review,
   `docs/PLAN.md` decision log 2026-11-05 (d)); a zone-fixed Feng cardiomyocyte direction (about 0 +- 0.3; not
   recommended; 2026-11-17: that estimate was the AI assistant's and no read stood behind it. The eighth pass
   reopened the line, Feng's statistics hold the zones at both ends, and it is read as try T53). Scenarios kept as risks and not built (closed levers): T1 depth and the oesophagus-junction cells; the
   heart's core-focused s2 (scope; a frozen rule); stack-limited embryo geometry (z extent; a frozen rule); T3's
   composition magnitude (a frozen rule).

The captain's upload gate (2026-11-05; `docs/PLAN.md` section 8): no first upload on any board until our estimated
leaderboard total is above 209 (about 184 today) or his deadline exception opens (the s1 methods freeze at 2026-11-27
00:00 UTC; from then the first round may go in, each upload only on his go-ahead at the time), so every entry stays
open to tries until then. The gate is about uploads only: it is never evidence for a try, and a try's row still names
its own expected gain on its counting backtests.

Once the heart and embryo s1 are uploaded, the frozen s2 rules build their s2 as s1, at s1's commit
(`docs/S2_RULES.md`), so from then on no try reaches those two boards unless the captain changes a rule.

Commit order: this row, N12 and the register rows; then the extraction code, the modules and N12 on a clean tree; the captain's sign-off on this gate (decision log); N12's run and results; last, the build commit, which pins the union's sha256 and flips the default. Fixed here, one form, no sweep: every cell is projected on the anchors' joint 30 PCs, and each bracket's PCs are residualised on log panel counts within that bracket. Strata are k-means with k = 20 on the anchors' residualised PCs (lloyd, n_init = 1, random_state 20261109), every cell assigned to its nearest centre. Within each stratum and bracket a cell's weight is w = exp(-s^2 / (2 var(s))), var(s) that stratum's variance of s (a stratum with no spread stays flat, weights floored at 1e-12), where s is the projection of the cell's residualised-PC offset from the local midpoint onto the local bracket-difference direction: the mean, over the 15 coupled anchor pairs nearest the cell's moved position, of (1 - t) f_i + t f_j, and the normalised mean of f_j - f_i, with partners the moves coupling's barycentres. By the Gaussian-product rule each stratum's draw moves about half way to the local midpoint. Each bracket keeps its nominal count exactly and each stratum its share of the bracket (vec.core.quotas); draws are weighted without replacement, with repeats only if a stratum's quota exceeds its pool; random streams 8 and 9. A tilt is a composition change within each bracket, which is legal on the embryo (its composition is not in the post-exposure block). A negative-binomial VAE (scvi-tools SCVI: n_latent 20, gene dispersion, observed library, no batch key, 400 epochs, batch 128, seed 20261109, CPU, 16 threads) is trained once per task on all the cells of its two brackets' panel counts, with no target and no external data, and encodes each cell (encoder means). For each fgw output cell and its coupled partner (`interpolate_clouds(with_partners=True, with_coupling=True)`, fgw's own draws, partners and positions): z_t = (1 - t) z_b + t z_a + sqrt(2 t (1 - t) (1 - rho)) eps, eps ~ N(0, S), t the pseudo-task's nominal fraction, S = (1 - t) S_b + t S_a the within-bracket covariance of each bracket's encoder means over its cells, rho the coupling-weighted correlation of the anchors' latent means on the coupling that set the moves (the trace of the cross-covariance over the root of the product of the two covariances' traces). The noise term restores the variance a pair average loses ((1 + rho) / 2 of V at t = 1/2 when the two brackets' variances are equal). NB counts are sampled at the decoded rate with each output cell's library taken from the real cell its row drew (its panel counts; never a decoded mean, PLAN principle 1) and normalised as the released files are, log(1 + 1e4 c / N), N the sampled panel counts plus the real cell's counts outside the panel, so the population's depth distribution stays the mixture's own and the closed depth line is not touched. Position is fgw's McCann position for the drawn cell. One form, no sweep. The model is trained once per task, so the 10 seeds vary pairs and NB draws only and training randomness is unsampled. Why it may not repeat E8: the latent is a model of states, not a count average, and the noise term restores latent variance; whether it reproduces a real population is what N18 checks at the interpolation's ends | Facet: which states emerge and their expression. A lineage under 200 reference cells, and one B0 does not move (Untracked, or fewer than 20 cells at a stage), is reported, not judged. The number of directions is reported per lineage and pair. The rule, fixed here (`t2_build.CFM_RULE`): for a lineage with at least 200 reference-stage cells with counts, the log1p CP10k of those cells is residualised, gene by gene, on a polynomial of degree 5 in the standardised log panel counts per cell (the depth axis, with the intercept; this removes it, as the review required), divided per gene by the residual SD and clipped at +-5 (no rescaling after). K_lineage is the number of eigenvalues of that covariance above the Marchenko-Pastur edge (1 + sqrt(p / n))^2, p the genes that vary among those cells and are not held flat, n the residual degrees of freedom (cells minus the fitted terms); nothing is tuned per lineage. The momentum divided by the same per-gene SD is projected onto those eigenvectors and multiplied back by the SD. Norm rule: the filtered factor is renormalised to the lineage's unfiltered L2 norm over the genes the filter acts on, so the candidate is not a partial hold. K = 0, or fewer than 200 reference cells: the lineage keeps B0's factor; a gene that does not vary among those cells keeps its factor. Held-flat handling: as B0, Untracked cells (a stage-tagged type with no cross-stage momentum) and lineages with fewer than 20 cells at a stage are never filtered; B0 on T2 passes no held-flat genes, and a held gene would be left out of the filter and the norm and stay at f = 1. The knockout file's own sections measure that instability, and an empirical-Bayes posterior mean (prior mean 0 on the log ratio, the no-change prior) shrinks unstable genes towards f = 1 more than stable ones, which suits ranking metrics (de_score, a top-list overlap; de_direction, a rank partial correlation). (a) Selection, the same in the test build and the backtests, nothing fitted on any transition's truth (the predicted-change table of the first draft is dropped: its change axis was scope-confounded on E9.5 -> E10.5): level bin = rank of the FULL reference's mean log1p CP10k, highest first, edges 100, 200, 400, 800, 1600, 3200; change bin = rank of dp = pb(prediction) - pb(full reference), most negative first: 0-399 the tail, 400-1599 near it, the middle, top 1600 predicted up. Eligible: level rank below 800 (the first four level bins; the thinning's first-order solve meets the band there at T1's depth), change bin near-tail or flat (never the tail, never predicted up), pb(reference) >= 0.25, not held (R2's union and the pre-registered list). Order: the level bin alone, ties by the lowest dp, then name; the first 200 whose target is reachable are promoted (200 is a constant, half the strong-null down list; no test count enters it). (b) Realisation (`apply_down_prior`, after M1's steps, `apply_factors` keep_depth=True, stream [seed, 4]): only the cells M1 moves along Feng's direction; each promoted gene's dp goes to the band -0.15 by one per-gene factor f >= 0.5 in the covered cells (genes needing less are skipped), solved from the exact first-order slope of binomial thinning with three fixed-point steps for keep_depth's constant; other genes move only by that constant. (d) Target-free checks in the build, the file written only if all hold: exactly 200 genes promoted; the scorer's mmd_unbiased(file after, file before) on the panel at most 0.0035 raw (at most 5000 cells, the same before and after; the covered cells' mean shift in 30 PCs of the pre-prior file reported, deciding nothing); at least 95% of promoted genes realise dp <= band + 0.02; expected library of every covered cell unchanged (exact), realised mean library ratio within 1%; uncovered cells and held genes exactly unchanged; other panel genes' mean log1p moves within -0.01 and 1.5 x the largest log depth constant + 0.01. If a check refuses, no strong-null file is written, T1's files stay M1 with R1 and R2 as built, the numbers go to the captain and no constant is relaxed (a change is a new row). If the regime does not transfer about -0.2 to -0.8 T1 by the scorer's formulas (mmd_u about -0.1 to -0.6 at d_f 0.08-0.11, more at a smaller d_f, the test's being hidden; variogram about -0.02; de_direction about 0 to -0.1; de_score only if n_dn exceeds about 150), against the approved -0.33; part (c) measures it | None counts for the test regime: no backtest can show the bet's payoff, and the regime at E12.5 is the unknown. Stop rule fixed now (`stop_rule.py`), means of the 10 paired differences: S1, on any of the three tasks the total board points or the distribution and covariance groups together (mmd_u 0.30, variogram 0.20) below -0.66 (twice the approved -0.33; break-even moves from about 16% to about 27%, the top of sheet C's 0.2-0.3); S2, on the control T1/E8.5->E9.5, raw mmd_u rise above 0.0035, raw variogram rise above 0.0004, or the two groups together below -0.33 points. Points are not comparable across tasks because the skill map's slope is the operating point's: one raw unit of mmd_u costs -52.4 points at b0_scope's operating point on the control, -61.0 and -68.6 at b0's on the E9.5 -> E10.5 tasks, -68 to -94 near the floor at d_f 0.11 to 0.08 and -312 near the floor at the control's d_f 0.024, hence raw units on the control. 0.0035 raw mmd_u is 0.33 points near the floor at d_f 0.08 (-1.09 at d_f 0.024, -0.53 at 0.05, -0.24 at 0.11; -0.18 at the control's own operating point); 0.0004 raw variogram is 0.36 points near the floor at d_f 0.0055 (-2.22 at 0.0009, -0.67 at 0.003, -0.20 at 0.01; -0.34 at the control's operating point). After the preflight, a within-type read that cannot be scored fails the whole gate, and the tries count. Gate (T13-T15, stage gate of late_proxy.py): lp_proxy passes the adoption rule's per-task test against b0_scope (paired mean gain above 0.3, ahead in at least 8 of 10 seeds) on H and on at least one within-type E10.5 read; on each read it passes, it also beats Cflip and Cperm by that test and its margin sits on the rank metrics (the de_recovery and direction margins together above 0 and at least half of the total margin, a coded condition of the pass: a fit stops the variants); neither control itself passes it on H; lp_proxy loses more than 0.3 on average on none of the three within-type reads. Variants (T16-T18, stage variants, run only if the gate passes, T18 also only if N20's stop lets it; a variant not run does not count): T16 and T18 against lp_proxy and T17 against T16 each pass only with mean gain above 0.3 and at least 9 of 10 seeds ahead on H and on both within-type E10.5 reads, the margin on the rank metrics on each, and no within-type mean loss above 0.3. Seeds check draw noise only (the ledger's opening), so the seed bar is no multiplicity correction; multiplicity is carried by the try count, stated in every report, and by asking for the gain on all three within-type reads. Landing, fixed now: no outcome changes an upload by itself, and since no counted task can run M1's real direction (E9.5 -> E14.5), a change to it can land only by the captain's ruling. Gate fails: M1's direction stays as ruled, nothing is recommended, T16-T18 are pruned unrun and uncounted; the row reads as no counted support for the transfer, which says little about the real bracket (a different, later biology). Gate passes: counted support that a Feng-bracket direction transfers to T1's platform, consistent with the smoke's known concordance on H and not independent of it; M1's direction stays as ruled. T16 passes: R2 (D, A1) has its first counted support and stands; below N20's similarity floor this is platform evidence only. T16 not passing: R2 stands by his ruling of 2026-11-06 (e); only a mean loss above 0.3 of T16 against lp_proxy on H goes to him as evidence against D and A1, his call. T17 passes against T16, at or above the floor: recommended to him: turn on R2's N1 and N2 lines for M1's real direction (configs/t1_hygiene.yaml lines, T1's s1 and s2 rebuilt through the build's hygiene path and hashed by 24 Nov, T1's s2 back to him under DATA_POLICY 2026-11-04 (d)); below the floor: platform evidence only, no recommendation. T18 passes, at or above the floor: recommended to him: the same rule (feng_dirs.eb_shrink with its deciles, in a new build option, default off) on M1's real direction, the same rebuild route; below the floor: platform evidence only, no recommendation. Each lands only by his ruling. If several pass, each is a separate recommendation, and their combination is not run. Shrinking a whole expression decile by its average half-disagreement should pull down the deciles whose changes are mostly noise relative to the better-measured ones, which could sharpen de_score's and de_direction's reading of the largest changes; the real bracket's small E9.5 groups (CM 705 cells, endocardial 70, epicardial 13 at E9.5) make it noisier than the proxy's, which is why the same step is of interest there. Metrics: de_score then de_direction, within type. | T20 | T1 | `g_mom_flip` (harness candidate, try T20, a sign control of T11; `vec.harness._g_mom_flip`, `vec.t1_state_transport.flip_factors`): g_mom's own draws, B0 base, the fold's fit and stream [seed, 5] (`_g_mom_arm`, the same calls as `_g_mom`, pinned byte-identical under an identity transform by tests/test_g_mom_verify.py), with every finished per-metacell within-type log factor's sign reversed (log f becomes -log f for every metacell and gene; zeros stay zero and the clip is symmetric); R1's held types and the pre-registered held-flat genes as in g_mom | copy_last; candidate g_mom (T11) is read against it, b0 and the floor reported; no entry changes | distribution (mmd_u, 0.30): -0.08 / 15.08; covariance (variogram, 0.20): -1.45 / 11.45 (the groups on which T11 gained) | T11's +1.13 and +1.23 must survive controls before any ruling (decision log 2026-11-10 (e)). What a sign reversal can say: it keeps every factor's size exactly and inverts only its direction, so it asks whether the gain is odd in the sign of the factor. A finished factor is the metacell's own deviation minus its transported partners', so it is partly a self-amplifier: any state-aligned effect is sign-odd at first order (g_mom expands the state contrast, the flip contracts it by the same amount), so g_mom minus the flip is about twice g_mom minus b0 whether the effect is momentum, amplification of the reference's existing states or a repair of the mix along them. This reports, before any ruling, what the factors would be at test, how far their direction resembles the backtest's against what a split of the same data can reach and what a fit with no development produces, and how much of them is the amplification of each state's own deviation or sits on the technical modules R2 holds flat in Feng's direction | None to any entry. Per fitted category of the test pair: the finished factors' size on the free genes (not the held-flat list): the cell-weighted mean over metacells and genes of abs(log f), the mean and 90% quantile over the entries not zeroed, the share of entries zeroed and at the clip, the number of metacells; the share of the category's E10.5 cells edited and held by R1, the held labels, and the share of free genes with any non-zero factor in any metacell; each edited cell's expected depth after application against its own, per category and over all edited cells (the largest absolute deviation of the ratio from 1, computed with the keep-depth constant `vec.core.apply_factors` returns for these factors; allowed 1e-9, the run stops otherwise), and the realised depth ratio (median, 1%, 99%) after one application on stream [20261110, 5]; the amplification of each fit (test pair, and backtest pair on the categories in both): the regression slope and R^2 through the origin of the finished factors on the metacell's own deviation (metacells cell-weighted, genes equal weight; the deviation is the log of the metacell's own mean CP10k, pseudocount 0.5, minus its cell-weighted mean over the category's metacells); the share of the factor size (l2 and l1) carried by R2's union genes among the free genes (`experiments/t1_hygiene/hygiene.py` `load_union`; not available if the union file is refused), and the number of union genes in the fit; for the categories in both fits, the concordance of the per-category factor direction with the backtest's fit (the absolute cosine of the leading right singular vectors of the two per-metacell factor matrices, each metacell weighted by the square root of its size, free common genes matched by name, and the Spearman correlation of the per-gene cell-weighted factor standard deviations over the genes with a non-zero size in either fit), the same two statistics after projecting the amplification component out of each fit, split-half ceilings (half-data estimates, each fit on half of each stage's cells, so they under-estimate the full-data ceiling: one random halving within every category; default_rng([20261110, 0]) for the test pair and [20261110, 1] for the backtest pair, start stage first; the geometric mean of the two pairs' axis ceilings as the ceiling for the pair-to-pair concordance), a no-development reference (the same two statistics between fits of two random halves of one stage, E10.5's with default_rng([20261110, 2]) and E9.5's with [20261110, 3]: what these statistics read with nothing developing), and the no-development fit's size against g_mom's fit per pair (the ratio of cell-weighted root mean squares over the free genes: the size T23's control is scaled up from). Against the backtest's fit, the leading axis has |cos| 0.20 (cardiomyocytes) and 0.29 (endocardium), above the no-development references of 0.03 and 0.01 but far below the split-half ceilings of about 0.91 and 0.84; projecting each metacell's own deviation out leaves 0.04 and 0.08. | H10 (the learned-forecast arm, added after a forwarded side note and kept by the captain's word; one try) | heart | `b0_lat_flow` (`campaign/gen`, commit 459916f; `vec.latent_extrap`, its `FLOW_*` constants and `FLOW_RULE`; written by an agent of the AI assistant to a specification the AI assistant fixed before the code, and reviewed against it). Row H9's vehicle to the last detail (the same fitted model in the same process, the same drawn cells, the same `ratio_counts` call and random stream, so that with equal latent moves the two outputs are equal bit for bit) with one difference: where H9 moves a cell by 0.5 of its lineage's mean last step, H10 moves it along a velocity field learned from every transition between consecutive input stages of the task. The field: one network for all moved lineages (the latent and a one-hot lineage in, three hidden layers of 256, a latent velocity out; no time input), fitted by flow matching on pairs drawn from an entropic optimal-transport plan within each lineage between consecutive stages (`vec.t1_state_transport.sinkhorn_plan`, at most 2,000 cells a side, 8,000 pairs per block), each transition weighted within a lineage by its gap in days, so that every day of observed development counts equally (0.17, 0.21 and 0.62 on the one-day backtest's three transitions; 0.44 and 0.56 on the other's two), 4,000 Adam steps on the CPU with fixed seeds; then integrated from each drawn cell's own latent over B0's time budget, 0.5 of the last gap, by RK4 in 8 steps. Every constant is fixed in the module; none is tuned and there is no option. The weights were corrected once, before any scored run and before the arm's build-only smoke was read: the first specification weighted a transition by the square of its gap (0.06, 0.09 and 0.85), called inverse-variance weights for a velocity whose error is a sample's offset divided by the gap. A side note forwarded by the captain pointed at the 85% on the step B0 already uses (decision log 2026-11-13 (q)), and the check showed the reason did not hold: adjacent transitions share a sample, so their errors are not independent, and under that model the pooled velocity's variance on gaps of 0.2, 0.25 and 0.75 days is 3.56 (the last step alone), 1.93 (squared weights), 1.39 (gap weights) and 1.21 (the least-squares optimum) times the offset's variance. With gap weights a lineage's mean velocity is its total latent change over all observed stages divided by the total time. The arm's own build-only smoke on the one-day backtest, started at the earlier weights, was still fitting its count model when this row was committed; the tests (planted flow recovered at the reference cells in sign and size, the encoder reproducing a real fit's training latents, the arm equal to H9 bit for bit when handed H9's latent move, the target never read) pass on the DGX at 459916f, and the scored run records the same target-free diagnostics per build | copy_last; s1 H1 and s2 (the half trim), unchanged whatever the outcome; candidates `b0` (the adoption base) and `b0_lat_ratio` (row H9, the reading this arm exists for) | as row H9: cell_state (0.25), expression change (0.25), neighbourhoods through content | A side agent's note, forwarded by the captain (decision log 2026-11-13 (l)): rows T34, T35, H8 and H9 reuse B0's forecast, so a loss there shows only that model-made or model-edited cells do not beat real cells; it does not show whether a model that learns how cells develop predicts better. H10 is the smallest arm that tests that on a board with more than two input stages. What it is, said plainly (the design panel's critic, decision log (n)): read at the reference cells such a field is the smoothed incoming velocity of the last step plus what cells in similar states did at earlier stages: a cell-by-cell form of B0's step, pooled over transitions, with a state-dependent part of the class H7 scored in count space (not kept). A limit the weights do not remove: where the stages' cells do not overlap in the count model's latent (it has no batch key, so each sample's own measurement profile moves its cloud), the field at the reference cells is set by the last transition alone, and the earlier transitions enter only through what the network shares across the latent. The design panel put its chance of passing the adoption rule on the heart at 0.03 to 0.07. Against it too, from `docs/CHALLENGE.md` ("Reference rows"): the organisers' own T1 reference of this family (`neural_ode`, an autonomous field in gene space, flow-matched on a global entropic optimal-transport coupling) "does NOT beat the constant shift", and their constant-velocity fits land below copy_last on every setting; H10 differs in fitting within lineages, in a count model's latent, and in editing real cells instead of writing model output. A second forwarded note advised limiting the arm to the heart, which was already so (decision log (o)); the captain then said "keep the heart arm and continue" (decision log (p)). The panel's own design had a step that removed each sample's measurement profile; it is left out (it removed about 9% of a profile sitting on the reference stage of the one-day task and revived the pruned "reversal of the heart's last step") | Expression only; coordinates, number, composition and size are B0's, the shape terms identical to `b0`'s. If a learned, state-dependent forecast adds over the lineage's mean step: mmd_u and the variogram up (states inside a lineage move differently), de_score and de_direction up where the pooled field's mean differs from the last step's mean, and by more on the 1.75-day backtest, where the last step is a quarter of a day and carries most sample-to-sample offset. Expected against `b0_lat_ratio`: between -0.3 and +0.3 on each backtest. The AI assistant's chance that the reading below is met: 0.07; that the arm is kept against `b0`: 0.04 | The two heart backtests, 10 seeds, in the same processes as rows H8 and H9. Two readings, both fixed now. (1) Against `b0` by `run.py decide --base b0 --change b0_lat_flow`: the adoption rule on both backtests; only this can lead to a request. (2) Against `b0_lat_ratio`, the reading the side note asks for: the learned forecast "adds" only if `b0_lat_flow` beats `b0_lat_ratio` by a paired mean above 0.3 with at least 8 of 10 seeds ahead on both backtests; otherwise the record says "no support that a learned forecast adds over B0's forecast in this vehicle at B0's budget". Validity, fixed now: the two arms' `latent_sha256` must be equal on each backtest (one fitted model), and the run's validity conditions of row H9 hold. Target-free, recorded by the run for every build (`latent_records/`): per lineage, the cosine and the size ratio between the field's mean displacement and B0's latent step, and the share of the field's displacement that varies within the lineage; if the median cosine is above 0.9 and the within-lineage share under 0.1, the arm is labelled "B0's step through a smoother" and a tie with H9 is the expected result, not a finding. The field's parameter checksum is recorded per build too; if it is not the same on every seed of a task (a draw that lacks a moved lineage refits the field), the row's outcome says so. Kill, fixed now: a loss of more than 1 point against `b0` on both backtests closes "an autonomous per-lineage latent flow-matching field on optimal-transport pairs, read at B0's budget on B0's cells in E14's count model" on the heart. It closes nothing wider: not a field with a time input, another count model, another budget, or a model trained on outside data. Counted under rule 4: heart 8 to 9 | the gain against H9 is on one backtest only; or it sits on mmd_u and the variogram alone while the within-lineage share is high (structured spread, H7c's finding: the no-development control moved every metric the same way at half the size); or `b0_lat_ratio` trails `copy` on a backtest (the vehicle's move hurts there) while the field's displacement is smaller than B0's (median size ratio r under 1) and the arm's gain over `b0_lat_ratio` is no more than (1 - r) of `copy`'s lead over `b0_lat_ratio` (a partial hold, which moves toward the copy); or the two arms' models differ (`latent_sha256`), which voids the reading | this row's commit; the code at 459916f on `campaign/gen` | **Run with rows H8 and H9 (459916f, clean tree, the same processes and the same fitted models: `latent_sha256` equal across the three arms on each backtest; one field per backtest, its parameter checksum the same on every seed), 10 seeds; counted (heart 8 to 9). Validity holds. Not kept, and reading (2) is not met.** Reading (1), `b0_lat_flow` against `b0`: `T2h/<=E9.5->E10.5` +1.18 (ahead in 10 of 10 seeds), `T2h/<=E8.75->E10.5` -0.17 (ahead in 1 of 10): the adoption rule passes on one backtest of two, no veto; not kept, and no request. The kill criterion is not met (no loss above 1 point). Reading (2), against `b0_lat_ratio`: +0.26 (ahead in 9 of 10) and +0.66 (10 of 10): ahead on both backtests, but under the 0.3 this row fixed on the one-day backtest, so by the row the record says: **no support that a learned forecast adds over B0's forecast in this vehicle at B0's budget.** Said beside it, because the miss is 0.04: the arm was ahead of the ratio arm in 19 of 20 seeds. What the arm was, from the run's target-free records: its mean move per lineage points along B0's last step (median cosine 0.99 on both backtests; 0.96 to 1.00 per lineage) and is 1.18 and 1.07 times its size, and 0.27 and 0.29 of its movement varies from cell to cell within a lineage. By the row's label rule it is not "B0's step through a smoother" (the within-lineage share is above 0.1), but it does not use the earlier stages either: the corrected weights (0.17, 0.21 and 0.62; 0.44 and 0.56, as recorded) left the direction where the squared weights had it (the build-only smoke at the squared weights, read after this row was committed: cosine 0.99, size 1.15, within-lineage share 0.30). That is the limit this row stated before the run: where the stages' cells do not overlap in the latent, the last transition sets the field at the reference cells whatever the weights. So the +0.26 and +0.66 over the ratio arm measure a somewhat larger and cell-specific last step, not a forecast learned from the ladder, which is what the third forwarded note said the test would measure. Where the difference to the ratio arm sits, on both backtests: de_direction (+0.009 and +0.008 skill), de_score (+0.008 and +0.016) and the variogram (+0.010 on each), with mmd_u and neighbourhoods within 0.007; so the row's second warning (spread metrics alone) does not apply, nor the partial-hold one (the move is larger than B0's, not smaller). Mean board scores: 51.61 and 51.28 (`b0` 50.43 and 51.45; `b0_lat_ratio` 51.35 and 50.62). What it leaves: of the candidates read against `b0` on these two backtests (H7, H8, H9, H10) it is the only one not vetoed on either, and it is still a gain on one backtest only, the row's first warning; H7 gained on the other one. No upload changes and none is requested. Not closed by this row: a field with a time input, a latent in which the stages overlap, another budget. The code stays on `campaign/gen`, off main. Results: `experiments/harness/results/gen_heart_v1/` (`against_ratio/` for reading (2)) |

So at the test pair the mirrored coupling is not one of two equal optima that a start could choose between: it is where the solver ends from both starts, at an objective 3.4% under the aligned coupling's. The idea's premise fails where it was needed, as the code's reviewer had found on a synthetic pair. For the record, deciding nothing: a coupling preferred this consistently may be anatomically right, with the two samples' coordinate frames mirror images of each other, which a proper rotation cannot align; an alignment that allowed a reflection would then be the fix. It is not registered as a try: on the E7.5 pair it would change nothing (partners are at random there), on the E7.25 pair it would restore thickness in the five mirrored draws, which is what lost Dice for row E11, and at the test it would give the first upload the unflattened geometry the second upload already has. | H13e, H13r and H13h (one row; three tries; the kept trend shrunk, made robust and carried over the horizon; the sixth campaign round, `docs/PLAN.md` decision log 2026-11-13 (dd) and (ee)) | heart | `b0_trend_eb` (H13e), `b0_trend_ts` (H13r) and `b0_trend_h` (H13h), harness candidates on the branch `campaign/trend2` (commit a0f3b5b, written by an agent of the AI assistant to a specification the AI assistant fixed before the code, and reviewed against it by a second agent; `tests/test_b0_trend.py`). Each is `b0_trend` to the last detail (the same draw, cells, coordinates, random stream, usable-stage rule and count-space step, through the same function) with a different per-lineage, per-gene log factor. **H13e:** the least-squares slope shrunk toward zero by its own sampling noise: within a lineage the genes are split into ten equal bins by their mean log level over the usable stages; per bin the residual variance of the fits is pooled, v = pooled variance / Sxx is the slopes' sampling variance, t2 = max(0, mean squared slope - v), and every slope of the bin is multiplied by t2 / (t2 + v); the time budget is `b0_trend`'s. **H13r:** the Theil-Sen slope, the median over all pairs of usable stages of the pair's slope, in place of the least-squares slope; the same budget. **H13h:** the least-squares slope carried over half the prediction horizon, 0.5 x (target day - reference day), instead of half the last gap; a lineage that keeps B0's own factor is scaled the same way. A lineage usable at only the last two stages keeps B0's own factor in H13e and H13r, as in `b0_trend`. Count space only; no constant beyond these; the test builders are untouched | copy_last; s1 H1 and s2 (the half trim), both with the trend since row H11; candidate `b0_trend` is the base of `decide`. A kept arm changes the heart's two files only by the captain's ruling and would need a fourth rehearsal | expression change (0.25) and cell state (0.25), as rows H11 and H12 | A cold review of this round's conclusion (decision log (ee)) found that the AI assistant's pre-run odds under-called both changes the adoption rule has kept: row T11 was priced at +0.2 to +0.8 with a 15 to 20% chance and scored +1.13 and +1.23; row H11 was given a 0.03 chance and scored +0.36 and +0.48. So arms that were pruned or never registered on such odds are read where a counted backtest exists and the read costs minutes. The three reasons, each of which holds at the test. (e) With three to five stage means per lineage, a gene's slope carries sampling noise that is larger, against the slope, for sparsely detected genes; the research report's "ladder line" (`experiments/research_2026_11_runs/REPORT.md`) named this shrinkage and it was never run. (r) Each sample carries its own per-gene profile (diagnostic N23), which acts on a slope like one outlying stage; a median of pairwise slopes resists one outlier where least squares does not. (h) B0's budget, half the last gap, is a habit of the last-step form; the targets lie 1 and 1.75 days ahead on the backtests and 2 days at the test, so half the horizon is what a rate would be carried over: 0.5 against 0.375 days on the one-day backtest, 0.875 against 0.125 on the other, 1.0 against 0.5 at the test | Expression (each lineage's step). Expected against `b0_trend`: H13e between -0.2 and +0.3 on each backtest; H13r between -0.3 and +0.3; H13h within 0.2 of zero on the one-day backtest and between -1.5 and +1.5 on the 1.75-day one, where the move is seven times larger. The AI assistant's chance that an arm is kept: 0.08, 0.06 and 0.05, stated with the caution that such odds were wrong twice. E31a balances the brackets in one greedy pass so that each position's 15 nearest positions hold the mixture's share as closely as the pass allows; displaced cells swap in least-distance pairs. E31b is the opposite: one bracket per patch, with round(1/min(p, 1-p)) k-means patches (random_state 20261109, the midpoint draw's) and one split patch; at the backtests' shares of one third and two thirds that is three patches of about a third of the cells each, so it reads segregation at the scale of the whole embryo. E31c permutes rows at random within greedy groups of 15 nearby positions, whatever their bracket or type. k = 15 is the scorer's neighbourhood (`NBHD_K`); streams are [seed, 21], [seed, 22] and [seed, 23]; one value each, no sweep. No arm looks at the scorer's row prefix or subsample: the arrangements are random or spatial only. Written before the run, from the reviewer's synthetic checks: on a segregated draw E31a moves cells many neighbourhood radii (45% of cells, a median of about 13 radii), so a loss can come from cells and their types leaving fgw's mapped positions and not only from the neighbourhood mix; and E31c's late greedy groups are not all local (about 5% of groups reach beyond 4 radii). Per lineage and gene the stage means are fitted monotone in the last step's direction by pool-adjacent-violators; a last step that is pooled away or zero gets factor 0; an unpooled step whose sign agrees with the mean slope of the other lineages gets the mean of the two slopes; any other gene keeps b0_trend's factor. R9's "half" is read as B0's damping, which is already in the step. B0 with its draw, shares, count, scope, momentum factors and held-flat genes unchanged, and one idea: where a factor is above 1 the rise is drawn as Poisson((f s - 1) m), with m the cell's library times the mean within-cell proportions of its 15 nearest cells of the reference stage (itself included) in 30 components of log1p CP10k, in place of the cell's own counts, so that zeros can rise. The constants are T3's frozen kernel's (15 neighbours, 30 components); the solver differs (a sparse one, to keep the whole transcriptome sparse). The depth constant s is solved exactly so that each cell's expected total is unchanged (T1 depth is closed; realised totals stay within 1% on average). Cells are redrawn one after another on B0's stream, so the arm's draws are not B0's except where the idea is idle; with a flat momentum the arm is B0 bit for bit. The same branch's other arm, the tail decoder, is being rebuilt after its review and gets its own row | copy_last; s1 and s2 as built (rows R6 and R7), unchanged; candidate `b0` is the base of `decide`. | T39 (one try; a recorded form of the tail decoder that try T38 left unread, named by an independent audit of the sixth round; decision log 2026-11-13 (ii)) | T1 | `b0_tail_profile` on the branch `campaign/t1-c` (commit 355363d, on top of 1976b6b of `campaign/t1-bfix2`; written by an agent of the AI assistant to a brief fixed before the code, and reviewed by a second agent, who re-derived the solve and checked it on a synthetic case of its own: "ready"; `vec.t1_scope.apply_tail_decoder_profile`, `tests/test_t1_batch_c.py`). `b0_tail` (try T38) with one change, the kernel. The selection is T38's own code, shared by both arms: a panel gene among the 400 most negative of the predicted change, with a reference level below 0.25, a negative predicted change and not held flat. A selected gene is lifted with try T37's neighbour-profile kernel in place of the cell's own counts: its count becomes c + Poisson((f - 1) m), m the cell's library times the mean proportions of the 15 nearest reference cells of the cell's source row in 30 components (`vec.t1_scope.topup_profile`, `vec.core.apply_factors_profile`), so zeros can rise. The factor is solved as T38 solves it, with this kernel's first-order slope (the mean over cells of m times the log1p step of one more count), bounded at T38's 2; a gene needing more, or with no profile mass, is skipped and counted. Each cell's expected library is kept. With nothing lifted the arm is B0 bit for bit. Each build prints one check line (numbers of genes and factors only). Two things the review noted, written here before the run: the solve is first-order in the log factor, as T38's is, so a lifted gene ends slightly above flat (about a fifth of its fall at half the bound, more near the bound), never below; and the profile is taken on the cells after the momentum step, where try T37 took it before (the same neighbours; the library differs by draw noise only) | copy_last; s1 and s2 as built (rows R6 and R7), unchanged; candidate `b0` is the base of `decide`. The T1 files change only by the captain's ruling | DE recovery (de_score, 0.25): diagnostic N3's line. Not T1's weakest group; a recorded form left unread, run as a counted try with its row first under rule 8 of `AGENTS.md` | Try T38 came out idle: every infeasible gene needed more than twice its own counts. Its row then closed the line by inference ("the kernel that can raise them is try T37's, which costs on the covariance term and gains nothing on the ranking"), but T37 applied that kernel wherever a factor is above 1 and never to the infeasible tail genes alone, and the ledger's own pruned entry names "a variogram-costly top-up of scope-driven genes" as the decoder's form. A rise spread over the neighbour profile lands mostly on zeros, where one more count moves log1p the most, so a gene needs a smaller factor under this kernel than under the own-count one and may fall within the bound | Expression only. If genes are lifted: de_score up a little, with the variogram and mmd_u flat or slightly down (T37's cost, on a few genes). E37b `fgw_geoshares`, exponential-growth state shares: `fgw` draws its cells from the two brackets at random, so each state's share of the draw is the linear mix of the brackets' shares; here the target share of a state present in both brackets is proportional to p_before^(1 - t) p_after^t, a state present in one bracket keeps its linear share, and the bracket counts stay exactly the entry's. The bracket-by-state table is the one closest in relative entropy to the entry's own with those margins (iterative proportional fitting), and the draw is the entry's own edited the least: rows dropped at random where a group's quota fell, unused rows of that state and bracket added where it rose. Every cell keeps its real expression and the position `fgw`'s moves give it. Where the target equals the linear shares by construction (every shared state with the same count ratio, tested on integers) the arm is `fgw` bit for bit. The heart's form of the decoder (try H17b), which fits T3 because both arms change expression by a per-gene factor: K13a `raw_tail` is the knockout factor's log ratio with every negative entry on a down-infeasible gene set to 0, then the factor's own last steps (rescale, clip); a gene is down-infeasible if the fold's reference level (the mean over the reference's cells of log1p CP10k) is below 0.25, the scorer's threshold. No kernel, no bound, no solve. With no such gene, or none with a negative entry, the arm is `raw` bit for bit. K13b `raw_tail_ctrl`, the control: the same filter on as many feasible genes with a negative entry as the arm changes, drawn at random on a stream of its own, so that the two change disjoint genes. The control first specified permuted the infeasible mask within deciles of the reference level; its builder and reviewer both showed that it was the arm itself on 90 to 100% of its genes (the mask is defined by that level), so the clause against it could never pass; it was replaced before any output existed. The second reviewer showed that a count-matched control moves far more of the factor's mass than the arm (the rescale differs) and no longer answers its question when it falls short; the reading was tightened for the second and the record carries the numbers for the first | wt_identity; T3's four built files, unchanged. | E41a and E41b (one row; two tries; two lines the record had closed because an update rule or a constant was on no record: Potts sorting, not built in row E31, and a partial FGW coupling, not built in row E32; a check by two independent agents of every such closure found a form with no free number for the first and split on the second; decision log 2026-11-13 (ii)) | embryo | Harness candidates on the branch `campaign/emb-f` (commit fcc1f6b, on top of f29a3eb of `campaign/emb-d`; written by an agent of the AI assistant to a brief fixed before the code, and reviewed by a second agent: "ready"; it recomputed the neighbour statistic from scratch after each of 113 swaps (no mismatch), compared the partial coupling with an independent call of the library (identical; transported mass as set; rows with no mass exactly zero), compared the new streams with every stream in use, and timed both arms at real size; `tests/test_emb_batch_f.py`), scored by their runner `experiments/harness/n45_embryo_f_arms.py` on the nine practice tasks (the floor, the two arms and the entry `fgw` rescored under its own name; one task per process; an arm the builder refuses on a task is recorded as not buildable there). E41a `fgw_potts`: the entry with one change, which drawn cell sits at which of the file's positions (cells, rows, positions and counts are the entry's). H is the mean over the file's cells of the share of their 14 nearest other positions that hold a cell of the same `group` (the scorer's neighbourhood size; the label level try E15 used); the target H* is H interpolated at the task's nominal fraction between the two brackets, each measured on a uniform draw of as many of its cells as the file holds, at their own positions; a cell and one of its 14 neighbours are swapped if and only if that moves H strictly closer to H*, until H reaches H* or a full round of proposals accepts none. No temperature and no coupling constant. E41b `fgw_partial`: the entry with one change, its coupling: POT's partial fused Gromov-Wasserstein on the entry's costs and alpha, transporting the share s of the mass, with s the overlap of the two brackets' anchor `celltype` distributions (the sum over labels of the smaller share); an anchor's displacement is its row's barycentre minus its own position and an unmatched anchor does not move; with s = 1 it is the entry. **Three points of E41b are choices that no record fixes** (the verifying agent's objection, kept here): the label level, the overlap as the estimate of the share, and the zero displacement of an unmatched anchor; no other value of any is tried. Written before the run, from the build and its review, so that none of it is read into the outcome afterwards: (1) the Potts rule is greedy and freezes: from a start far from its target it stalled at 0.40 to 0.42 against a target of 0.95 on synthetic clouds of 2,000 and 30,000 cells, so where the entry's file is far from H* the arm's change is set by where the rule freezes and not by the interpolated share; a rule that does not freeze needs a temperature, which no record gives; next to the target the run can also end one swap short, which counts as reached. (2) H* is measured on bracket cells at their released positions and H on the entry's moved and rescaled file, so the direction of the swaps can come from that difference in geometry (on the synthetic stages the entry's file was above both brackets and the arm lowered the share). The build records' stop field and gap to H* are read per seed before the scores. (3) For the partial coupling, POT has one iteration cap for its outer loop and its inner solves, and the entry's outer cap of 200 makes the inner solve fail from about 300 anchors; the arm passes the entry's inner cap and lets the outer loop stop at POT's tolerance, the only reading that builds; every other solver argument is POT's default. (4) With s exactly 1 the arm is the entry, which is rare on real labels | copy_last; s1 `fgw` and s2 `fgw_aligned_series`, unchanged. T43b, the mixture's control: every cell is moved with the factor 1 + w (F - 1), w = (n // 2) / n and F the factor B0 applies to that entry; nothing is recomputed. T43c: the step is made with `apply_factors(kernel='multinomial')`, the heart arm `b0_trend_mn`'s kernel unchanged. It matches the mixture's expected counts exactly, gene by gene and cell by cell, and not what the scorer reads: in the pseudobulk of log1p CP10k its step is log(1 + w (F - 1)), which is never below w log F, so it moves up genes further and down genes less (about 0.54 and 0.46 of B0's shift on synthetic data, against 0.50 for the mixture on both). (2) In that pseudobulk the mixture is exactly linear, w of B0's shift on every gene, and its unmoved half is the unshifted chamber copy, so on the de terms it is a half-size step to first order; only the cell-distribution term sees that half the cells moved fully. (3) The brief's premise for T43c was false in what the scorer reads. The redraw keeps every cell's library exactly, but the per-cell keep-depth constant makes every free non-zero count a moved one, so it redraws every count of every cell and adds about one count of variance per count; in log1p CP10k that acts like thinning to about 0.6 of depth (synthetic: pseudobulk shift -0.25 on genes the step leaves alone, correlation 0.95 with a binomial thinning profile, detected share 0.57 to 0.49). Row T40's sentence that count noise is the depth lever on T1 holds for this arm. Its two check-line fields are constant by construction. (4) The half is uniform over all drawn cells and not stratified by category, so per category the moved share varies by seed. (5) The brief gave the control as (1 + F) / 2; with an odd count the mixture moves n // 2 of n cells, and the code's w matches that. | H22 (one try; two stopping points on one swap path; the heart's part of the ledger's pruned T2 line "Potts sorting extrapolation", read on the embryo as try E41a and never on the heart, which the seventh independent sweep of 2026-11-14 named open; decision log 2026-11-13 (ii)) | heart | `b0_trend_potts` and `b0_trend_potts_d` on the branch `campaign/heart-f-fix` (commit 26e7010, on top of b62db9a of `campaign/heart-f` and fcc1f6b of `campaign/emb-f`; written by an agent of the AI assistant to a brief fixed before the code; reviewed by a second agent, two must-fix findings about the brief, fixed and reviewed again by a third: "ready"; `tests/test_heart_batch_f.py`). Each is `b0_trend`'s file to the last detail (cells, expression rows, positions, number, size) followed by one step: which drawn cell sits at which position is sorted by try E41a's zero-temperature swap rule, unchanged (`potts_sort`, the scorer's neighbourhood of 15, stream 30): a cell is swapped with one of its nearest positions if and only if that moves H, the mean share of a cell's nearest positions holding a cell of the same lineage label, strictly closer to a target. Labels are `obs['merged']`, the lineage level `b0_trend` works at, with its untracked label counted as one label. H of each input stage is measured on a uniform draw of as many cells as the file holds (stream 29), and a least-squares line of H on stage day is fitted over the task's input stages. `b0_trend_potts` takes the line's value at the target's day as its target (the form the sweep named, as try E41a reads the brackets' line at the target's day); `b0_trend_potts_d` takes H of the file as drawn plus the slope times B0's damping times the last gap (the entry's own time budget). No constant beyond these. **Written before the run, from the reviews.** (1) The two forms are not two ideas: they share one swap path and differ only in where they stop; when both stall, or both targets lie beyond reach, their files are identical. They are read as one dose-response and count as one try. (2) The brief's validity check was wrong: above 1,500 cells the pinned scorer's cell-distribution terms subsample the prediction's rows by index, so a permutation changes which rows they read; any change of the cell-state group is subsample noise, the effect is read on the local-organisation term only, and the shape terms must equal `b0_trend`'s exactly. (3) H of the file and H of its reference stage differ by an independent draw (about 0.01) and, where the reference holds fewer cells than the file, by a repeat bias of about +0.02; the line form's target carries both and a level offset from the fit, so it is never idle, and on the 1.75-day backtest it reads a line fitted over half a day 1.75 days ahead and may simply run to the greedy stall; the damped form is near-idle there (a budget of 0.125 day) and its damping was chosen on that task. (4) H mixes lineage shares with arrangement, so a slope that comes from changing shares, or from the change of sampling between E9.5 and E10.5, is applied as sorting; an untracked-excluded and a composition-corrected form are not run. Each arm is `b0_trend`'s file (cells, coordinates, number, size, the per-lineage trend factor F and its streams) with one addition: a per-cell, per-gene factor D inside each moved lineage, applied with F in `b0_trend`'s one count-space call. Each drawn cell of a moved lineage has a latent z in try H10's count model (the model and its fit unchanged: one fit per task, shared by the four arms and by every seed) and a learned move dz over H10's time budget (B0's damping times the last gap). The move is centred within the lineage's drawn cells: du is dz less its mean over them. r is the decoder's mean at z + du over its mean at z, its log clipped at log 3 as H10's ratio arm clips it. D = r / G, with G iterated until each gene's expected count total over the lineage's drawn cells after `b0_trend`'s depth rule is what it is without D (to 1e-12 relative). Where `b0_trend` does not move a lineage, or where every cell has the same dz, D is 1 and the arm is `b0_trend` bit for bit. The four arms differ only in where dz comes from. H23a: H10's field (`fold_flow` and `flow_latent`, every `FLOW_*` constant unchanged). H23b, its placebo: the same after each cell's within-lineage residual (its z less the mean of its lineage at its true stage) is permuted across the stages within its lineage and each stage's own lineage mean is added back, by one seeded permutation: it has seen each lineage's mean development and no within-lineage development. H23c, the form H10's row left open: every cell's latent centred on the mean of its lineage at its stage, with H10's pairs, weights, network and constants on those centred latents over every transition between consecutive input stages, so that transitions before the last can inform the reference cells. H23d, its placebo: the same on the centred residuals permuted across stages within lineage. No new constant: the recentring's tolerance and iteration cap are float settings. **Written before the run, from the two reviews.** (1) D leaves each moved lineage's expected count total per gene after `b0_trend`'s depth rule unchanged. It does not hold the scored pseudobulk, which is the mean over cells of log1p CP10k and moves through the within-lineage spread (about 1 to 2% of `b0_trend`'s own change per gene on the reviewer's synthetic task). Every moved cell's counts are a fresh draw, uncorrelated with `b0_trend`'s at the same build seed (46.6% of moved non-zero entries equal, against 45.7% for `b0_trend` at another seed). A difference from `b0_trend` on de_score or de_direction is therefore read as resampling noise unless it exceeds `b0_trend`'s own seed-to-seed spread, and never as a mean-level effect. (2) Each placebo removes within-lineage development together with every other stage-specific within-lineage structure: stage-wise depth, which substates each stage's sections sampled, and the encoder on stages outside its fit. Its D is generally smaller than the arm's (sd of log D on synthetic data with a depth trend and no biology: 0.0171 against 0.0127; with a pure change of substate proportions: 0.0191 against 0.0141). An arm leading its placebo shows that the field uses stage-specific within-lineage structure, not that it learned development, and since the two differ in size a lead may be a dose effect. A placebo is not a field near zero: the entropic blur makes every field a contraction toward the lineage's centre. (3) The pooled form learns how each lineage's centred latent cloud changes shape between stages. That includes section sampling of substates and stage-wise technical differences, which neither it nor its placebo can tell from development; its pairs couple positions relative to the lineage mean, not states. (4) Coordinates and drawn cells are `b0_trend`'s, so the shape and growth group cannot move; expression change, cell state and local organisation can. D keeps each cell's expected total: it is not a depth change, and `b0_trend` already redraws the counts of every cell it moves. A gain on the cell-state or neighbourhood terms that the placebo shares is smoothing, not development. (5) Each task has one count-model fit, one field per arm and one placebo permutation. The ten seeds resample cells and counts only, so seed counts are not evidence about the fit, the field or the permutation; the two backtests are the independent units, and they share one truth stage. (6) H23a is H10's field in procedure only: the run refits the count model in a new process, so its field is H10's fitted field only if the fit reproduces. The run's `latent_sha256` per task is compared with the committed ones of `results/gen_heart_v1/latent_records/`, and the outcome says which it was. Inputs: the three released T1 files and Feng's statistics at the bracket's two ends only (no other Feng stage is read). For each of M1's direction groups, over the genes M1 moves: the change between two sets of cells is the log ratio of their mean CP10k with the config's pseudocount, Feng's own formula; the share is the least-squares multiple of Feng's five-day change that fits it, s = sum(change x d) / sum(d x d); the corrected share divides by the direction's reliability between the two halves of Feng's cells, computed in this read over the same genes. Shares: (a) E8.5 to E9.5; (b) E9.5 to E10.5 library 1, 2, 3 and pooled; (c) inside E10.5, libraries 1 and 2 to library 3, and library 1 to library 2. Scale: per pair of groups, the slope of the challenge's E9.5 contrast between the two groups on Feng's E9.5 contrast, its median over the pairs L, corrected for the contrast's reliability L_corr. A = the mean of the combined shares of (b) for libraries 1 and 2 (the day where the kind does not change); K = library 3's minus A; P = (a) over the groups with their own cells at both stages. Courses for the test's two days: the bound f_max = L - A, an even pace from E10.5 to E14.5 f_even = (L - A) / 2, and a pace that decays by a constant factor per day f_decay. A bootstrap over the challenge's cells (200 draws) gives each value its 5th and 95th percentiles. Self-check: the same reading on a world built from E9.5's cells alone, where half of them are moved by M1's own step at 0.2 and stand in for E10.5's portion libraries, and the other half's contrasts stand in for Feng's, so that the day share is 0.2 and the scale 1 by construction; the measured minus known values are the read's bias. **Written before the run, from the reviews**, in their words where quoted. (3) "L is the size of between-type contrasts at E9.5 in the challenge's data relative to Feng's; using it for the within-type change over five days assumes one scale for both, which the read cannot test; the E10.5 library 3 against E14.5 slope and each pair's correlation are reported as the only check." (4) "K counts any difference between the heart library and the embryo libraries that lies along Feng's direction, ambient heart RNA included (2% soup gives about 0.13 on synthetic data); a depth difference alone gives none. (5) "A is read on the embryo libraries alone; any difference between E9.5's and E10.5's embryo portions (dissection, composition within a label) that lies along Feng's direction is read as pace and subtracted from K." (6) "L is lowered by any impurity of the challenge's labels or any pooling of Feng's labels (10% mislabelled cells lowered it by a fifth on synthetic data), and A and L rest on different groups and weights; an 'above both courses' can come from L alone." (7) "The courses assume the same E9.5 and E14.5 in both datasets, one scale for all genes and groups and a course that never turns back; they are two courses among many, and a statement about 0.4 says nothing about scores (T44 found larger steps score better)." (9) "The self-check stands Feng in with the challenge's own E9.5 cells, so its bias holds the pseudocount, M1's count-space step and the cell sampling of one dataset at one point, not the depth difference between the two datasets nor label impurity or pooling; a statement that survives the widening can still be decided by those." (11) "0.4 is compared with the courses in share units; M1's own 0.2 step reads as s_cal in those units, so the fraction M1 would need is the course times 0.2 / s_cal, which is reported and decides nothing; neither unit says what scores." What holds without them is the shape (the peak at 4 times as released, at 6 times at two thirds, beyond 8 at one third, the same in the second draw, and as libraries 2 and 1 lie) and D33 on the raw metrics (de_score +0.15, de_direction +0.003, mmd_u -0.011). What it says for the step's question (decision log 2026-11-17 (c)): on the deepest released truth, which is also the one of the test pair's kind, the candidates' worst-case multiples (3.0 for the entry's 0.40; 4.1, 4.4 and 5.1 for 0.55, 0.60 and 0.69) all lie on the flat top and none below the entry's (58.52, 58.49 and 58.39 against 58.22 by interpolation; no read multiple between them is behind 3 times), so each passes test A; at the low end of the expected range the interpolated gains over 0.40 are +0.70, +0.79 and +0.95; and because the turn follows the truth's depth, test B caps the advice at 0.60.
